    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Pythia↔OLMo 91-92% retention (architecture-agnostic). Qwen-rewrite catastrophic transfer to other models (61-63%), but Qwen-cont much better (80-85%). 0v1 universally transferable (>0.96 AUC), 1v2+ transfer depends on model family. 的行为，发现 K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变，展现出跨模型架构、跨解码策略、跨生成模式的 universality。\\n\\n**子组件：**\\n\\n**(a) K*=2 universality across architectures, generation modes, and decoding strategies**\\n> **4/4 model-mode 1v2 range [0.6866, 0.7039]，K* 完全不变\\n> - K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）\\n> - ⚠️ θ-sensitivity: θ=0.60 下 4/4 K*=2；θ=0.70 下仅 Config4-T12 K*=2（1v2=0.7039 仅超 \\\"suggestive\\\" 而非确定性结论。Config4-T12 正确 top-3 features: hapax_ratio, ttr, surp_skew（非 freq_entropy）\\n> - ⚠️ K*=2 边缘性双端注意: Qwen-cont 1v2 CI lower=0.6015（+0.15pp 高于 θ）+ Qwen-rw 2v3 CI upper=0.5991（-0.09pp 低于 θ），K*=2 边界在两端都极边缘。Discussion/Limitations 需同时提及\\n> - Paper 可写：\\\"K*=2 is established as a consistent discrimination boundary across all 8 tested conditions (4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies; bootstrap 95% CI for AUC₁ᵥ₂ > 0.60; AUC₂ᵥ₃ CI upper < 0.60), suggesting universality beyond the ≤1.5B scale studied.\\\"\\n> - ⚠️ 措辞策略（D017 Reviewer 评估）：分两层——\\\"established as consistent\\\" 用确定性语言（8/8 有统计检验支撑），\\\"suggesting universality beyond\\\" 对外推 hedging。不升级为 \\\"establishing universal\\\"——3 模型 ≤1.5B 会引发审稿人质疑消耗 rebuttal 预算\\n> - ⚠️ C1' 子组件定位：呈现为\\\"对同一 claim 的 4 个 evidence 维度\\\"而非 4 个独立 finding。§4 标题用 \\\"K* Characterization\\\"，每个子节开头显式回答一个 reviewer concern，子节间用 transitional sentences 链接\\n\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\n> - transfer retention 80-92% suggests depth signal has a large text-intrinsic component（不量化具体比例，因 transfer accuracy retention ≠ 信号分解）\\n> - ⚠️ 数值精度：Pythia→OLMo 91.6%, OLMo→Pythia 90.3%，范围 90-92%（非 91-92%）；Qwen-cont↔Pythia 84.7%/85.2%，范围 84-85%（非 83-85%）\\n\\n**(c) Degradation ordering consistency**\\n> - Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85 on single-feature AUC ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\n\\n**(e) Representation dependence of K* (§4.x Neural Probe / §5.x Discussion)**\\n> - exp-015 完整 3 模型结果（frozen self-embeddings + MLP probe, each model probing its own generations）:\\n>   - Qwen-cont: K*(neural)=3, 2v3 AUC=0.6159, CI [0.6099, 0.6223], CI lower > 0.60 ✅\\n>   - Pythia-1.4B: K*(neural)=2, 2v3 AUC=0.5963, CI [0.5892, 0.6033], CI lower < 0.60 ❌\\n>   - OLMo-1B: K*(neural)=2, 2v3 AUC=0.5892, CI [0.5818, 0.5965], CI lower < 0.60 ❌\\n> - **K*(neural) 不具备 universality**：仅 Qwen-cont 达到 K*=3，Pythia/OLMo 仍为 K*=2\\n> - **Self-familiarity 解释**：每个模型用自己的 frozen embeddings 探测自己生成的文本。Qwen 对 Qwen-generated 文本更敏感（self-familiarity），K*=3 是 model-specific artifact 而非 universal signal\\n> - **K*(theory) ≥ 3 弱化**：只能 claim \\\"K*(theory) ≥ 3 for Qwen representations\\\"，不能泛化为所有模型\\n> - **Interpretability–deployability tradeoff 加强**：15D features 给出 universal K*=2（3/3 模型一致），neural probe 给出 model-dependent K*∈{2,3}。Model-free 方案的 universality 优势在 cross-model 结果下更明显\\n> - 所有 3 模型 neural 2v3 AUC > lgbm 2v3 AUC（+3.9pp 到 +5.5pp），说明 learned representations 确实恢复了额外 depth signal，但不足以在所有模型上推过 θ=0.60\\n> - 论文定位：**正面发现**——cross-model validation 揭示 K*(neural) 是 representation-dependent，验证了选择 model-free 15D features 作为 headline K* characterization 的正确性\\n> - 预防审稿人 \\\"why bury the stronger result?\\\"：K*(neural)=3 仅对 Qwen 成立且是 self-familiarity driven，不适合作为 headline result；15D K*=2 的 universality（8/8 conditions + 3/3 models）才是可靠可部署的结论\\n\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (K*-Aware Data Curation Validation)\\nK*=2 correctly predicts that graduated refinement beyond the binary boundary provides no additional benefit: binary K*-aware filtering (0.5941 MMLU) outperforms graduated depth weighting (best 0.5892 at α=0.9), surprisal-based selection (0.5894), and DSIR distribution matching (~0.5867, ≈ no-filter level). K* serves as an actionable curation guideline — the discrimination boundary directly determines the optimal filtering granularity.\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. K*=2 correctly predicts that graduated refinement beyond the binary boundary provides no additional benefit — binary filtering at K* (0.5941 MMLU) outperforms all tested alternatives including graduated weighting, surprisal selection, and DSIR.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n1. **\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → K*=2 恰好预测了这一结果：binary filtering at K* boundary (0.5941 MMLU) outperforms graduated weighting (best 0.5892), surprisal selection (0.5894), and DSIR (~0.5867). 这验证了 K* 作为 actionable curation guideline 的价值——discrimination boundary 直接决定了最优 filtering 粒度。K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → **exp-015 直接回应**：frozen LLM embeddings + MLP probe 达到 K*=3（vs 15D K*=2），揭示 representation-dependent hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D。Hand-crafted 15D features 的优势在于 model-free transferability（80-92% cross-model retention）、可解释性（支持 degradation ordering 分析）、实用可部署性。Neural representations 多辨别 1 个深度级别但代价是 model dependence + ~100x 计算开销。这一 tradeoff 证明 15D features 是 practical choice，同时表明 theoretical boundary 延伸至 K*=2 之上\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\n9. **\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\" 升级为 \\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\n\", \"total_chars\": 11298, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

K* serves as an actionable curation guideline — the discrimination boundary directly determines the optimal filtering granularity.\\\\n\\\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\\\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\\\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\\\\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. K*=2 correctly predicts that graduated refinement beyond the binary boundary provides no additional benefit — binary filtering at K* (0.5941 MMLU) outperforms all tested alternatives including graduated weighting, surprisal selection, and DSIR.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\\\n\\\\n1. **\\\\\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\\\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\\\\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\\\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\\\n2. **\\\\\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\\\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\\\n3. **\\\\\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\\\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\\\n4. **\\\\\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\\\\\"** → K*=2 恰好预测了这一结果：binary filtering at K* boundary (0.5941 MMLU) outperforms graduated weighting (best 0.5892), surprisal selection (0.5894), and DSIR (~0.5867). 这验证了 K* 作为 actionable curation guideline 的价值——discrimination boundary 直接决定了最优 filtering 粒度。K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\\\n5. **\\\\\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\\\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\\\n6. **\\\\\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\\\\\"** → **exp-015 直接回应**：frozen LLM embeddings + MLP probe 达到 K*=3（vs 15D K*=2），揭示 representation-dependent hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D。Hand-crafted 15D features 的优势在于 model-free transferability（80-92% cross-model retention）、可解释性（支持 degradation ordering 分析）、实用可部署性。Neural representations 多辨别 1 个深度级别但代价是 model dependence + ~100x 计算开销。这一 tradeoff 证明 15D features 是 practical choice，同时表明 theoretical boundary 延伸至 K*=2 之上\\\\n7. **\\\\\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\\\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\\\n8. **\\\\\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\\\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\\\n9. **\\\\\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\\\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\\\n\\\\n## Venue Fit\\\\n\\\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\\\\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\\\\\" 升级为 \\\\\\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\\\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\\\n\\\", \\\"total_chars\\\": 11298, \\\"truncated\\\": false}, Looking at the review feedback, we're in the rebuttal phase with weak reject/borderline scores and three critical issues to address. The main concerns are around the theoretical justification for θ=0.60 (which I've already covered with Cohen's h and task-based arguments, just need the K*(θ) curve visualization) and clarifying the scorer-generator distinction through experiments with Pythia and OLMo models.

For the warning issues, I'm waiting on confirmation about the 7B model experiments, still need to add multiple comparison corrections with paired t-tests and Bonferroni correction to the analysis, and I've already resolved the permutation test scaling and self-BLEU leakage concerns through the ablation studies.

The remaining findings from FR-6 through FR-16 will be addressed in the paper revisions once the Agent finishes writing. I'm tracking a tight deadline with all rebuttal experiments due today, paper finalization tomorrow, and the ARR submission on the 25th, with one GPU available for the remaining computational work.

The Agent appears to be stuck—it's been idle for 72 minutes while still marked as processing, which is blocking progress on the experiments. I need to assess whether the 7B experiment (D028) can realistically fit within the timeline and GPU constraints, since running a 48-hour job on a single A6000 would conflict with the submission deadline if started now.

There's a contradiction in the documentation that I need to resolve: D028 claims the user has confirmed the 7B experiment is mandatory and should start immediately, but the ROADMAP still shows it as pending user confirmation. I should clarify which status is accurate before proceeding.

Looking at the experiment coverage, I'm counting 12 completed experiments and 6 planned ones, which adds up to the 18 total entries in the plan. The planned experiments are exp-004, exp-008, exp-009, exp-012, exp-014, and exp-016.

Now I'm checking the rebuttal experiments mentioned in the roadmap—EXP-A through EXP-E—and they don't have corresponding registry entries. EXP-B and EXP-C appear to have passed their tests, while EXP-A, EXP-D, and EXP-E are still in progress, but none of them show up in the registry system.

This is problematic because without registry tracking, there's no audit trail for these rebuttal experiments. I'm also noticing an internal contradiction in the roadmap where D028 states that the 7B model is mandatory and should start immediately, yet FR-4 still shows it's waiting for user confirmation. These need to be reconciled before moving forward.

Looking at the timeline, today is the deadline for completing all rebuttal experiments by 05-15, but EXP-A, D, and E aren't done yet. The GPU situation is concerning too—cuda:0 is idle and the Agent appears to be stuck. However, from a pure compute perspective, I have enough resources: about an hour for EXP-A plus 10 minutes for the K*(θ) visualization. The 7B experiment will take roughly 48 hours on the current A6000, pushing completion to 05-17, which doesn't block the 05-25 ARR submission deadline. The real risk is if the Agent stays frozen, that blocks everything. The experimental structure supporting the 2+1 claim framework is solid with sufficient evidence backing it up.

For the 7B experiment timing, running it before EXP-A would block the critical rebuttal work, so I need to clarify priorities. There's also a tool bug with the compute_coverage_matrix function—it's treating a list as a dictionary key—but I can work around that. I'm also noting that the Qwen-cont CI lower bound of 0.6015 for the 1v2 AUC case is the most marginal result, which the roadmap mentions but doesn't flag as problematic.

The reviewer's concern FR-1 directly targets this θ=0.60 threshold for lacking theoretical grounding, and while the response using Cohen's h and task-based arguments is solid, the K*(θ) curve figure is still pending. For exp-004, there's a discrepancy between the plan (8h estimate) and the roadmap (~48h)—the 7B model on a 48GB A6000 likely does take longer than the original 1.5B estimate, even with the smaller C4 1K docs scale.

I'm noticing the decisions file was truncated at D017, so I need to review the rest. Looking at the current status report, I can see several issues worth flagging: the ROADMAP has internal contradictions around the 7B status between D028 and FR-4, the rebuttal experiments lack registry traceability, the agent is stuck with idle GPU time right before the deadline, and there's this unresolved GPU budget discrepancy for the 7B model that could be the largest remaining allocation.

I should verify the GPU status independently and check if the rebuttal experiments EXP-B and EXP-C have results documented elsewhere. I also need to be careful about the unit conversion mistake noted in my memory—1pp equals 0.01, not 0.1. Once I've confirmed these details, I'll compile my findings for the Director.

Now I'm identifying the critical issues to flag: there's an internal contradiction in the ROADMAP where D028 indicates the 7B model should start immediately per user instruction, yet FR-4 still shows it's waiting for confirmation. Additionally, the rebuttal experiments EXP-A through E lack registry entries, which means even the passed experiments like EXP-B and EXP-C can't be traced back to their source data—this violates review standards and makes the paper's citations of these results unverifiable.

There's also an urgent GPU issue: today is the DDL for completing all rebuttal experiments, but EXP-A/D/E haven't finished and the Agent appears stuck with 72 minutes of idle time on cuda:0, though the Director is already aware and has issued a replacement queue. Finally, there's a significant discrepancy in the 7B GPU budget—the experiment plan shows 8 hours but the ROADMAP estimates around 48 hours, a sixfold difference that needs clarification on actual runtime for the 7B model on the A6000.

Looking at the overall direction and timeline, the 2+1 claim structure is well-supported by 12 completed experiments with solid evidence for K*=2 universality, and the remaining rebuttal experiments only need about 1-2 hours of GPU time excluding the 7B model, so compute isn't the limiting factor—the real DDL risk comes from Agent availability rather than raw processing power.

But there's also a hardware mismatch I'm noticing: the ROADMAP claims "1×A6000 48GB (cuda:0)" while nvidia-smi is reporting an RTX 4090 D with 24GB. That's a significant discrepancy that could affect whether the 7B model experiments will even fit in memory. The RTX 4090 D with 24GB is significantly tighter than the assumed 48GB A6000 for running Qwen2.5-7B in fp16, so I need to verify the actual GPU configuration and check what's currently consuming memory on the device. 还有个矛盾的地方——D028 说"确认必做，立即启动"，但 FR-4 那一行还是显示"⏸ 等用户确认"。另外，EXP-A 到 EXP-E 这些 rebuttal 实验都没有在 registry 里追溯记录，连已经 PASS 的 EXP-B 和 EXP-C 也找不到条目。关于 DDL 的算力评估，剩余 rebuttal 任务大概需要 1-2 小时（不算 7B），但 7B 实验在 ROADMAP 里估的是 48 小时，experiment_plan 里又说 8 小时，而且这两个估计都是基于错误的 48GB 显存假设，必须重新算一遍。

现在我意识到 GPU 硬件不匹配这个问题其实很严重——7B 实验可能根本跑不了。不过也有可能服务器上有多块 GPU，项目只能用 GPU 0 这块 4090 D。让我查一下是不是真的只有一块 GPU。从 nvidia-smi 的输出来看，确实只列出了一块 GPU（索引 0），所以服务器就是单块 RTX 4090 D 24GB，不是 A6000 48GB。这是个硬约束，直接影响 7B 的计划。现在我要开始写审查报告，但在那之前还想查一下 GPU 上现在在跑什么任务。
```

```text
Now I have the full picture. The project config says the GPU is an RTX 4090 D (autodl-4090d), not an A6000. The ROADMAP is wrong about the GPU type.

Key issue: ROADMAP says "1×A6000 48GB (cuda:0)" but actual hardware is RTX 4090 D (~24GB). D028 says "A6000 48GB fp16 可跑" for Qwen2.5-7B, but the card only has 24GB. This is a critical factual error that could lead to failed experiments.
 
For Qwen2.5-7B in fp16, I need about 14GB for model weights alone. On a 24GB 4090 D, this is tight but workable since exp-004 is inference-only (generating depth chains with C4 1K docs), not training—so with small batch sizes it should be feasible.

The actual pipeline breaks down into three parts: generating depth chains via Qwen2.5-7B (GPU-intensive), computing 15D features (CPU), and running LightGBM (CPU). The GPU bottleneck is just the generation phase, and while the 4090 D is actually faster than the A6000 in raw compute power, the 24GB constraint means it'll be slower overall and the time estimates in the roadmap might need adjustment.

I'm verifying the GPU allocation—the config confirms gpu_ids=[0] and the process is running on cuda:0, so there's no boundary violation. The real issue is that the ROADMAP lists "1×A6000 48GB" but the actual hardware is an RTX 4090 D with ~24GB, which is a factual error that needs correcting even if the 7B experiment is still feasible.

For the second point, I'm noting that the ROADMAP's status information is outdated—it claims the Agent is stuck with idle GPU, but EXP-A is actually running right now with the Pythia scorer processing Qwen-cont data on cuda:0. There's also a discrepancy between FR-4's status and D028's current state that needs clarification.

The bigger issue is that experiments A through E lack registry entries, which means their conclusions can't be properly traced back to source according to standards—even though B and C passed validation, they're still missing the documentation needed for paper citations. GPU allocation looks fine since EXP-A is correctly using cuda:0 within project boundaries, and the 2+1 restructuring toward K* characterization aligns with the deadline timeline.

Looking at the data support situation, I have 12 completed experiments covering all C1' subcomponents, with the remaining rebuttal GPU work needing about an hour (EXP-A is already running) and the 7B experiments requiring roughly 48 hours. The May 25th ARR submission deadline isn't a hard constraint on compute since even if 7B starts on the 15th and finishes by the 17th, there's still plenty of time for revisions. The real limitation is the 24GB constraint on the 4090 D, which caps what we can do if we need to scale up for faster results.
```

[System 触发审查]:
[Director 请求] 论文写作质量深度审视

用户明确指示：不要只盯着小改小动，要深度反思写作质量。之前的审稿修复偏重机械性问题（格式、数值、orphan bib）。请从以下维度做一轮深度审视：

1. **Narrative 论证链**：从 Introduction 的 motivation → Method 的 K* 定义 → Experiments 的证据呈现 → Discussion 的解读，整条逻辑链是否连贯有力？每个 claim 是否有足够的论证深度（不是简单报数字）？
2. **段落过渡与节奏**：章节间、段落间过渡是否自然？是否有"列数据不解释"的情况？Reader 能否顺畅地 follow story？
3. **Discussion 深度**：每个实验结果（特别是 negative results 如 graduated < binary、K*(neural)=[2,2,3]）是否有充分的 mechanistic 讨论？还是只停在"现象+一句解释"？
4. **Claim 与证据匹配**：当前 claim 强度是否与手头证据精确匹配？有无 overclaim 或 underclaim？
5. **对比写作水平**：与 EMNLP 2025/2026 同领域 accepted papers（AI text detection, data curation）相比，写作成熟度差距在哪？

重点：这不是一轮 bug-finding，是对论文整体写作成熟度的评估。请给出具体的、可操作的写作改进建议，标注优先级。

[System 触发审查]:
**请求：论文整体写作质量深度评估（不是逐条 issue 修复）**

用户要求我们不仅盯小修小补，而是从整体写作质量角度系统性反思。请你做一轮 holistic writing quality assessment，重点关注以下维度：

1. **Narrative coherence & persuasion**：从 intro 到 conclusion 的故事线是否流畅、有说服力？K*=2 universality 的 claim 是否被充分论证？reader 读完是否会被说服这是一个重要发现？

2. **Evidence sufficiency**：当前 3 个 claim（C1' K* characterization, C3 graduated filtering, generation mode analysis）的证据链是否完整？有没有明显的 gap 需要补大实验（而不是小 ablation）？特别关注：
   - ≤1.5B 规模限制是最大攻击面，是否需要 7B 验证实验来加强论文？
   - cross-model transfer 的 "text-intrinsic" claim 在替代 scorer 实验（EXP-A）之后是否足够强？
   - C3 graduated filtering 的 effect size 偏小，怎么处理才能让 reviewer 满意？

3. **Writing quality at section level**：每个 section 的写作质量（clarity, precision, logical flow）是否达到 EMNLP main 水准？哪些 section 需要重写而不是修补？

4. **What's missing for a strong accept?**：如果你是 EMNLP reviewer 给这篇论文打分，从 borderline accept 升到 accept 需要什么？是补实验、改 framing、还是写作质量整体提升？

请给出具体的、可执行的建议。大改（包括补大实验）不用回避——用户明确说时间和资源都够。DDL 5/25 还有 10 天，完全来得及。

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	The gap cuts across three communities.
11	Binary detection methods~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp, zawalski2026codec} produce no depth estimate.
12	The model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} characterizes recursive degradation at the macro level but provides no per-text depth estimation tool.
13	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.
14	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone, nor quantifies the boundary beyond which such estimation becomes infeasible.
15	
16	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$.
17	We empirically measure $\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations---using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
18	We operationalize $\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k$ where the bootstrap 95\% CI for pairwise $\auc_{k,k+1}$ exceeds $\theta = 0.60$.
19	
20	Our primary contribution is a systematic empirical characterization of $\Kempirical$:
21	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
22	  \item \textbf{$\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\approx}0.20$) across 4 model-mode configurations (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}) and a decoding strategy ablation on Qwen-rewrite covering 4 strategies (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}), with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\theta$ (permutation $p \leq 0.002$).
23	  \item \textbf{Cross-model transfer}: 80--92\% accuracy retention across continuation-mode generators; 0v1 $\auc > 0.96$ (range: 0.963--0.991) across all pairs.
24	  \item \textbf{Feature degradation consistency}: Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$ on single-feature rankings across continuation-mode models, with surprisal-variance features dominating.
25	  \item \textbf{Generation mode}: rewrite yields higher 1v2 $\auc$ ($+$8.4\,pp) but transfers poorly (61--63\% vs.\ 80--92\%).
26	\end{enumerate}
27	
28	\noindent As a secondary contribution, $\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering in downstream filtering: binary curation at the $\Kempirical$ boundary minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\ base $0.6018$), while graduated ($0.5892$) and surprisal-based ($0.5894$) strategies provide no additional benefit (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
29	
```

1	% EMNLP 2026 Main — ARR May 2026

24	\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}
25	
26	\author{Anonymous}
27	
28	\begin{document}
29	\maketitle
30	
31	% === Abstract ===
32	\begin{abstract}
33	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
34	We introduce a framework using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier to measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable.
35	At threshold $\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\approx}0.20$, a small-effect-size threshold), $\Kempirical{=}2$ across 4 model-mode configurations and a decoding ablation covering 4 strategies at the ${\leq}1.5$B scale: all 1-vs-2 bootstrap CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ while all 2-vs-3 CI upper bounds fall below it.
36	Cross-model transfer retains 80--92\% accuracy when features are computed by a fixed scorer (0-vs-1 $\auc{>}0.96$).
37	Neural probing yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen but 2 for Pythia and OLMo, establishing $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a feature-set-specific lower bound with model-dependent additional discriminability.
38	Frontier LLMs achieve ${\sim}88\%$ binary detection (GPT-5.5: 87.8\%; Claude Opus~4.6: 88.2\%) but only ${\sim}33\%$ five-class depth accuracy, confirming depth estimation is not solved by frontier LLMs in a zero-shot setting.
39	$\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering in downstream filtering: binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively among all tested strategies.
40	\end{abstract}
41

43	\input{introduction}
44

46	\input{related_work}

58	\input{discussion}
59	
60	% === Section 7: Conclusion ===
61	\input{conclusion}
62	
63	% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
64	\section*{Limitations}
65	
66	Our findings are bounded by (1)~model scale ($\leq$1.5B), (2)~prompt-chain rather than retrain-chain protocols (our $\Kempirical$ is a conservative upper bound on retrain-chain $\Kstar$ under monotonic-degradation assumptions), (3)~English C4 text only, (4)~four decoding strategies, (5)~threshold sensitivity ($\Kempirical$ shifts from 3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; our conclusions are conditioned on $\theta{=}0.60$), (6)~marginal boundary (Qwen-continuation 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.0015), (7)~the 15D feature set yields a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher), and (8)~the neural probe boundary is model-dependent ($\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen only; $2$ for Pythia/OLMo), likely reflecting self-familiarity rather than a universal signal.
67	Detailed analysis appears in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
68	
69	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
70	\section*{Ethics Statement}
71	
72	Generational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing---positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.
73	Our experiments use the publicly available C4 corpus and do not involve personally identifiable information.
74	A potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\auc > 0.99$ at 0v1) remains robust.
75	
76	\bibliography{references}
77	
78	\input{appendix}
79	\input{appendix_extended}
80	
81	\end{document}

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
7	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
8	\label{fig:pipeline}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
12	\label{sec:formulation}
13	
14	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
16	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
17	Together with Markov chain convergence, this guarantees a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations are indistinguishable.
18	
19	We define the operational lower bound:
20	\begin{equation}
21	  \Kempirical = \max\{k : \text{CI}_{\text{lower}}^{95\%}(\auc_{k,k+1}) > \theta\},
22	  \label{eq:kstar}
23	\end{equation}
24	where $\theta = 0.60$ corresponds to Cohen's $h \approx 0.20$ (a conventional small-effect-size threshold) and lies well above the permutation null distribution (95th percentile $< 0.55$ across all configurations; bootstrap: 1{,}000 resamples).
25	This choice is further validated by the downstream filtering experiment (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}): binary curation at $\Kempirical{=}2$ outperforms graduated strategies, confirming that $\theta{=}0.60$ captures a practically meaningful boundary.
26	$\Kempirical$ varies with $\theta$: at $\theta{=}0.55$, $\Kempirical{=}3$ uniformly; at $\theta{=}0.65$, continuation modes drop to $\Kempirical{=}1$; at $\theta{=}0.70$, $\Kempirical{=}1$ universally (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
27	
28	\subsection{Features and Classifier}
29	\label{sec:features}
30	
31	Each document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).
32	All features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).
33	Hand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer without retraining and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.
34	
35	We use \emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\text{depth} > k \mid \mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).
36	OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).
37	
38	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
39	\label{sec:experimental_setup}
40	
41	We evaluate four configurations spanning three model families (Table~\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B (continuation).
42	Each uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).
43	Default decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.
44	Continuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.
45	Evaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\auc$ as primary metric.
46	
47	\begin{table}[t]
48	\centering
49	\small
50	\caption{Experimental configurations.}
51	\label{tab:setup}
52	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
53	\toprule
54	\textbf{Config} & \textbf{Model} & \textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\
55	\midrule
56	Qwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\
57	Qwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
58	Pythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
59	OLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
60	\bottomrule
61	\end{tabular}
62	\end{table}
63	
```

      "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this guarantees a finite $\\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations are indistinguishable.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\{k : \\text{CI}_{\\text{lower}}^{95\\%}(\\auc_{k,k+1}) > \\theta\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\theta = 0.60$ corresponds to Cohen's $h \\approx 0.20$ (a conventional small-effect-size threshold) and lies well above the permutation null distribution (95th percentile $< 0.55$ across all configurations; bootstrap: 1{,}000 resamples).\nThis choice is further validated by the downstream filtering experiment (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}): binary curation at $\\Kempirical{=}2$ outperforms graduated strategies, confirming that $\\theta{=}0.60$ captures a practically meaningful boundary.\n$\\Kempirical$ varies with $\\theta$: at $\\theta{=}0.55$, $\\Kempirical{=}3$ uniformly; at $\\theta{=}0.65$, continuation modes drop to $\\Kempirical{=}1$; at $\\theta{=}0.70$, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ universally (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nHand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer without retraining and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate four configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B (continuation).\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",
      "numLines": 63,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 63
    }
  },

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate generational depth estimation on four model configurations:
5	Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B in continuation mode, and Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct in both rewrite and continuation modes.
6	All experiments use the OBD-LightGBM classifier with the 15-dimensional feature set (Section~\ref{sec:method}), with 5{,}000 texts per depth level (generations 0--5).
7	
8	%% ====================================================================
9	\subsection{$\Kempirical = 2$ Across All Configurations}
10	\label{sec:kstar_boundary}
11	
12	\begin{table*}[t]
13	\centering
14	\small
15	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ for adjacent depth pairs with bootstrap 95\% CIs and permutation $p$-values for boundary rows.
16	$\Kempirical = 2$ requires 1v2 $\text{CI}_\text{low} > \theta{=}0.60$ and 2v3 $\text{CI}_\text{high} < \theta$.}
17	\label{tab:kstar_ci}
18	\begin{tabular}{lcccccccc}
19	\toprule
20	 & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-rw} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Pythia} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{OLMo} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-cont} \\
21	\cmidrule(lr){2-3}\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\cmidrule(lr){6-7}\cmidrule(lr){8-9}
22	 & $\auc$ & 95\% CI & $\auc$ & 95\% CI & $\auc$ & 95\% CI & $\auc$ & 95\% CI \\
23	\midrule
24	0v1 & .9956 & & .9932 & & .9970 & & .9971 & \\
25	1v2 & .6955 & [.686,\,.704]\rlap{$^*$} & .6539 & [.645,\,.663]\rlap{$^*$} & .6145 & [.605,\,.625]\rlap{$^*$} & .6112 & [.602,\,.621]\rlap{$^*$} \\
26	2v3 & .5893 & [.580,\,.599] & .5514 & [.542,\,.563] & .5496 & [.540,\,.560] & .5609 & [.551,\,.571] \\
27	3v4 & .5435 & & .5152 & & .5091 & & .5409 & \\
28	4v5 & .5340 & & .5136 & & .5045 & & .5141 & \\
29	\bottomrule
30	\multicolumn{9}{l}{\footnotesize $^*$Permutation $p = 0.002$ for all 1v2 comparisons.}
31	\end{tabular}
32	\end{table*}
33	
34	\begin{figure}[t]
35	\centering
36	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_auc_decay.pdf}
37	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed). The horizontal dashed line marks $\theta = 0.60$.}
38	\label{fig:auc_decay}
39	\end{figure}
40	
41	Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} show a consistent pattern: 0v1 discrimination is near-perfect ($\auc > 0.99$), followed by a sharp ${\sim}$30\,pp drop at 1v2 ($\auc$: 0.6112--0.6955), with 2v3 falling below $\theta$ (0.5496--0.5893) and deeper pairs converging toward chance.
42	All four 1v2 CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ (most marginal: Qwen-continuation at 0.6019), and all four 2v3 CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$, with permutation $p = 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons.
43	This establishes $\Kempirical = 2$ as a consistent boundary across all tested configurations.
44	
45	The 1v2 $\auc$ varies across models: Qwen-rewrite (0.6955) $>$ Pythia (0.6539) $>$ OLMo (0.6145) $\approx$ Qwen-continuation (0.6112).
46	The spread due to generation mode within a single model ($\Delta = 8.4$\,pp) exceeds the spread across models in the same mode ($\Delta = 3.9$\,pp), suggesting generation protocol is a stronger determinant of detectability than model identity.
47	
48	\begin{figure}[t]
49	\centering
50	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_kstar_theta.pdf}
51	\caption{$\Kempirical$ as a function of threshold $\theta$ for four model-mode configurations. At $\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\approx}0.20$, dashed vertical), $\Kempirical{=}2$ uniformly. The boundary shifts to $\Kempirical{=}3$ at $\theta{=}0.55$ and $\Kempirical{=}1$ at $\theta{=}0.70$.}
52	\label{fig:kstar_theta}
53	\end{figure}
54	
55	Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta} shows $\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$: at $\theta = 0.55$, $\Kempirical = 3$ uniformly; at $\theta = 0.65$, continuation modes drop to $\Kempirical = 1$ while rewrite retains $\Kempirical = 2$; at $\theta = 0.70$, $\Kempirical = 1$ universally.
56	All four decoding configurations (greedy, nucleus, $T{=}0.70$, $T{=}1.2$) likewise yield $\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta = 0.60$, with 1v2 $\auc$ in $[0.6866, 0.7039]$ (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}).
57	
58	%% ====================================================================
59	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}
60	\label{sec:transfer}
61	
62	\begin{figure}[t]
63	\centering
64	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
65	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap (6-class ordinal accuracy). Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo achieves 90--92\% retention; Qwen-rewrite$\to$continuation-mode models: 61--63\%.}
66	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
67	\end{figure}
68	
69	Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap} shows the transfer accuracy matrix (full matrix in Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}).
70	Continuation-mode transfer is high: Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retains 90--92\% of within-model accuracy, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$Pythia 84--85\%, and Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$OLMo 80--85\%.
71	At the 0v1 boundary, cross-model $\auc$ exceeds 0.96 for all pairs (range: 0.963--0.991).
72	These retention rates suggest that depth features transfer across generators when computed by a fixed scorer.
73	
74	Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\% retention to Pythia/OLMo), confirming reliance on mode-specific artifacts.
75	Cross-mode transfer is asymmetric: continuation$\to$rewrite retains 89\% while rewrite$\to$continuation retains 82\%, indicating that continuation-mode features form a generalizable subset.
76	
77	%% ====================================================================
78	\subsection{Feature Degradation Ordering}
79	\label{sec:feature_degradation}
80	
81	\begin{table*}[t]
82	\centering
83	\small
84	\caption{Top-3 features by single-feature $\auc$ ranking. Bold: shared across all continuation-mode models. Spearman $\rho$ (continuation pairs): Pythia--OLMo 0.95, Pythia--Qwen-cont 0.91, OLMo--Qwen-cont 0.85.}
85	\label{tab:top_features}
86	\begin{tabular}{cllll}
87	\toprule
88	Rank & Pythia & OLMo & Qwen-rw & Qwen-cont \\
89	\midrule
90	1 & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}} & \texttt{freq\_entropy} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}} \\
91	2 & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d2\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}} \\
92	3 & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d2\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}} & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d2\_std}} \\
93	\bottomrule
94	\end{tabular}
95	\end{table*}
96	
97	Among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\rho \geq 0.85$; Table~\ref{tab:top_features}).
98	The top-3 features---\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}---are shared across all three families, all measuring surprisal variability.
99	This shared ordering suggests a common mechanism: each generation step smooths the token-level surprisal landscape.
100	Qwen-rewrite diverges, with \texttt{freq\_entropy} as its top feature---corroborating the cross-model transfer findings.
101	A limitation is that depth-pair-level agreement weakens ($\tau \approx 0.37$ at 1v2), indicating per-feature contributions at the boundary vary across models.
102	
103	%% ====================================================================
104	\subsection{Generation Mode and Neural Probe}
105	\label{sec:generation_mode}
106	
107	Within Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, rewrite mode yields 1v2 $\auc = 0.6955$ versus continuation's $0.6112$ ($\Delta = +8.4$\,pp).
108	The amplified detectability reflects instruction-following artifacts that are discriminable but mode-specific rather than transferable across generators, as confirmed by the poor cross-model transfer of rewrite-mode classifiers (61--63\% vs.\ 80--92\% for continuation).
109	Continuation mode is therefore recommended as the canonical condition for cross-model comparisons.
110	
111	\paragraph{Neural probe.}
112	Replacing hand-crafted features with frozen encoder hidden states (MLP probe; Table~\ref{tab:s7_neural_probe}), we evaluate $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}$ across all three model families (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
113	Qwen-continuation achieves 2v3 $\auc = 0.6159$ $[0.610, 0.622]$, yielding $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$.
114	However, Pythia ($\auc_{2v3} = 0.5963$ $[0.589, 0.603]$) and OLMo ($\auc_{2v3} = 0.5892$ $[0.582, 0.597]$) both fall below $\theta$, giving $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$.
115	The Qwen-only elevation likely reflects self-familiarity: each model's frozen embeddings probe its own generations, and Qwen's instruction-tuned representations encode mode-specific artifacts that boost boundary discrimination.
116	The 15D model-free features yield $\Kempirical = 2$ universally (3/3 models), validating their choice for cross-model deployment.
117

122	\label{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}
123	\begin{tabular}{lccc}

129	OLMo-1B    & 0.5892 & $[0.582,\,0.597]$ & 2 \\

Pythia$\\leftrightarrow$OLMo achieves 90--92\\% retention; Qwen-rewrite$\\to$continuation-mode models: 61--63\\%.}\n\\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap} shows the transfer accuracy matrix (full matrix in Table~\\ref{tab:s1_transfer}).\nContinuation-mode transfer is high: Pythia$\\leftrightarrow$OLMo retains 90--92\\% of within-model accuracy, Qwen-cont$\\leftrightarrow$Pythia 84--85\\%, and Qwen-cont$\\leftrightarrow$OLMo 80--85\\%.\nAt the 0v1 boundary, cross-model $\\auc$ exceeds 0.96 for all pairs (range: 0.963--0.991).\nThese retention rates suggest that depth features transfer across generators when computed by a fixed scorer.\n\nQwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\\% retention to Pythia/OLMo), confirming reliance on mode-specific artifacts.\nCross-mode transfer is asymmetric: continuation$\\to$rewrite retains 89\\% while rewrite$\\to$continuation retains 82\\%, indicating that continuation-mode features form a generalizable subset.\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Feature Degradation Ordering}\n\\label{sec:feature_degradation}\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Top-3 features by single-feature $\\auc$ ranking. Bold: shared across all continuation-mode models. Spearman $\\rho$ (continuation pairs): Pythia--OLMo 0.95, Pythia--Qwen-cont 0.91, OLMo--Qwen-cont 0.85.}\n\\label{tab:top_features}\n\\begin{tabular}{cllll}\n\\toprule\nRank & Pythia & OLMo & Qwen-rw & Qwen-cont \\\\\n\\midrule\n1 & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_std}} & \\texttt{freq\\_entropy} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}} \\\\\n2 & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d2\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_std}} \\\\\n3 & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d2\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}} & \\textbf{\\texttt{surp\\_d2\\_std}} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\nAmong continuation-mode models, single-feature $\\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$; Table~\\ref{tab:top_features}).\nThe top-3 features---\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}, \\texttt{surp\\_std}, \\texttt{surp\\_d2\\_std}---are shared across all three families, all measuring surprisal variability.\nThis shared ordering suggests a common mechanism: each generation step smooths the token-level surprisal landscape.\nQwen-rewrite diverges, with \\texttt{freq\\_entropy} as its top feature---corroborating the cross-model transfer findings.\nA limitation is that depth-pair-level agreement weakens ($\\tau \\approx 0.37$ at 1v2), indicating per-feature contributions at the boundary vary across models.\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Generation Mode and Neural Probe}\n\\label{sec:generation_mode}\n\nWithin Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, rewrite mode yields 1v2 $\\auc = 0.6955$ versus continuation's $0.6112$ ($\\Delta = +8.4$\\,pp).\nThe amplified detectability reflects instruction-following artifacts that are discriminable but mode-specific rather than transferable across generators, as confirmed by the poor cross-model transfer of rewrite-mode classifiers (61--63\\% vs.\\ 80--92\\% for continuation).\nContinuation mode is therefore recommended as the canonical condition for cross-model comparisons.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural probe.}\nReplacing hand-crafted features with frozen encoder hidden states (MLP probe; Table~\\ref{tab:s7_neural_probe}), we evaluate $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}}$ across all three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nQwen-continuation achieves 2v3 $\\auc = 0.6159$ $[0.610, 0.622]$, yielding $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$.\nHowever, Pythia ($\\auc_{2v3} = 0.5963$ $[0.589, 0.603]$) and OLMo ($\\auc_{2v3} = 0.5892$ $[0.582, 0.597]$) both fall below $\\theta$, giving $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$.\nThe Qwen-only elevation likely reflects self-familiarity: each model's frozen embeddings probe its own generations, and Qwen's instruction-tuned representations encode mode-specific artifacts that boost boundary discrimination.\nThe 15D model-free features yield $\\Kempirical = 2$ universally (3/3 models), validating their choice for cross-model deployment.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{Neural probe pairwise $\\auc$ with bootstrap 95\\% CIs (frozen-encoder MLP). $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen only; Pythia and OLMo match the 15D boundary at $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$.}\n\\label{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{2v3 $\\auc$} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} & $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-1.5B  & 0.6159 & $[0.610,\\,0.622]$ & 3 \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 0.5963 & $[0.589,\\,0.603]$ & 2 \\\\\nOLMo-1B    & 0.5892 & $[0.582,\\,0.597]$ & 2 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

```text
1	\section{Application: $\Kstar$-Aware Data Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts that graduated refinement beyond the binary boundary provides no additional benefit over binary filtering.
5	We compare six data curation strategies on a corpus of 30{,}000 documents (5{,}000 per depth, depths 0--5): (i)~\textbf{no-filter} (uniform weight), (ii)~\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\text{synthetic})$ from the 15D LightGBM classifier), (iii)~\textbf{binary-Drayson} (importance weighting following \citet{drayson2025}, same classifier, weights $w_j \propto (1 - q(x_j))^b$, $b{=}1$, cap $r_{\max}{=}3$), (iv)~\textbf{graduated} (exponential decay $w = \exp(-\alpha \cdot \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), (v)~\textbf{surprisal} (retain documents above corpus median surprisal), and (vi)~\textbf{DSIR}~\citep{xie2023dsir} (importance resampling against depth-0 reference).
6	
7	We fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via LoRA (rank~8, $\alpha_{\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs, evaluating on MMLU (14{,}042 examples, 0-shot) and HellaSwag (10{,}042 examples, 0-shot) across 5 seeds; the graduated strategy is further crossed with 5~$\alpha$ values (51 total runs).
8	
9	\paragraph{Results.}
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\caption{Downstream performance of six data filtering strategies. Fine-tuning: Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, LoRA, 30K documents, 5 seeds. Graduated shows best $\alpha$ only; full sweep in Table~\ref{tab:tab_alpha_sweep}.}
15	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
16	\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}
17	\end{table}
18	
19	All strategies degrade absolute performance relative to the base model (MMLU 0.6018, HellaSwag 0.6821); the goal is damage minimization rather than improvement.
20	Binary-ours minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941 \pm 0.0008$; HellaSwag $0.6701 \pm 0.0006$), reducing the MMLU gap from $-$1.53\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\,pp.
21	Binary-Drayson ($0.5913$; $0.6691$) and graduated filtering at its best $\alpha$ both fall short of binary-ours.
22	Surprisal-based selection (MMLU $0.5894 \pm 0.0009$; HellaSwag $0.6641 \pm 0.0006$) falls short by $-$0.47\,pp / $-$0.60\,pp.
23	DSIR performs worst among all strategies (MMLU $0.5869 \pm 0.0003$; HellaSwag $0.6631 \pm 0.0007$), only $+$0.04\,pp above no-filter on MMLU and $-$0.07\,pp below on HellaSwag---its bigram feature hashing captures domain shift but not the depth-correlated distributional signatures that our classifier exploits.
24	
25	\paragraph{$\alpha$ sensitivity.}
26	Higher $\alpha$ values yield monotonically better performance on both benchmarks (HellaSwag: $0.6640$ at $\alpha{=}0.1$ to $0.6701$ at $\alpha{=}0.9$).
27	At $\alpha{=}0.9$, graduated filtering narrows the MMLU gap to 0.49\,pp below binary-ours ($0.5892$ vs.\ $0.5941$) and matches binary-ours exactly on HellaSwag ($0.6701$)---consistent with higher $\alpha$ progressively approximating binary thresholding.
28	The fact that even the best graduated configuration does not reach binary-ours confirms that ordinal depth assignments for $d \geq 2$ introduce noise rather than signal (detailed $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
29	
30	\paragraph{Summary.}
31	$\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering: binary curation at the $\Kempirical$ boundary minimizes degradation most effectively, while finer-grained strategies (ordinal depth weighting, surprisal scoring, DSIR) provide no additional benefit---consistent with depth assignments beyond $k{=}2$ carrying no discriminative signal.
32	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Interpretation.}
5	The consistency of $\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ across all tested configurations (4 model-mode and 4 decoding strategies) indicates that the two-step information bottleneck is a structural feature of autoregressive generation at the $\leq$1.5B scale.
6	The feature degradation analysis provides mechanistic evidence: surprisal-variance features degrade in a shared order across model families (Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$), suggesting that each generation step smooths the token-level surprisal landscape in a model-independent fashion.
7	Cross-model neural probing reveals that $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen-continuation but $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo; the Qwen elevation likely reflects self-familiarity artifacts in instruction-tuned embeddings rather than a universal signal, so $\Ktheory \geq 3$ can only be claimed for Qwen representations (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_neural_probe}).
8	Extended theoretical discussion, including connections to model collapse theory and scale considerations, appears in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_discussion}.
9	
10	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
11	The implied three-tier taxonomy (human / generation-1 / generation-2+) requires no per-model or per-decoding-strategy calibration.
12	Cross-model transfer (80--92\% accuracy retention; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) enables deployment without generator-specific retraining.
13	Our filtering experiments (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) show that binary filtering at the $\Kempirical{=}2$ boundary is the most effective strategy among those tested; graduated strategies offer no additional benefit.
14	Frontier LLMs achieve ${\sim}88\%$ binary detection (GPT-5.5: 87.8\%; Claude Opus~4.6: 88.2\%) but only ${\sim}33\%$ five-class depth accuracy (below OBD's 38.6\%), confirming that depth estimation is not solved by frontier LLMs in a zero-shot setting (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_llm}); few-shot or fine-tuned LLMs may narrow this gap.
15	
16	
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We presented the first systematic measurement of the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.
5	At $\theta = 0.60$ (Cohen's $h \approx 0.20$), $\Kempirical = 2$ across 4 model-mode configurations and a decoding ablation covering 4 strategies at the $\leq$1.5B scale: all 1v2 bootstrap CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ (permutation $p \leq 0.002$) while all 2v3 CI upper bounds fall below it.
6	Cross-model transfer retains 80--92\% accuracy when features are computed by a fixed scorer, with 0v1 $\auc > 0.96$ across all pairs.
7	Neural probing yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen-continuation but $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo, establishing $\Kempirical = 2$ as a feature-set-specific lower bound; the Qwen elevation reflects self-familiarity rather than a universal depth signal.
8	
9	The $\Kempirical = 2$ boundary correctly predicts the strategy ordering in downstream filtering: binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively among all tested strategies, while all approaches (including binary-ours at $0.5941$ MMLU) remain below the base model ($0.6018$).
10	
11	Key directions for future work include scaling to 7B+ models, extending to multilingual and multi-domain corpora, establishing the prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain $\Kstar$ relationship, and developing model-agnostic neural probes that overcome the self-familiarity limitation.
12	
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model Collapse.}
5	Shumailov et al.~\cite{shumailov2024collapse} showed that iterative training on model outputs causes irreversible tail-distribution degradation.
6	Alemohammad et al.~\cite{alemohammad2024selfconsuming} proved convergence to a fixed point, and Dohmatob et al.~\cite{dohmatob2024model} derived scaling laws for tail probability loss.
7	Schaeffer et al.~\cite{schaeffer2025position} organized the literature into eight definitions; our $\Kstar$ aligns with their Family~B (D4: collapsing variance, D6: mode entanglement).
8	Kazdan et al.~\cite{kazdan2025collapse} distinguished replace and accumulate regimes; our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime, making $\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.
9	These works characterize macro dynamics but provide no per-text depth estimation tools.
10	
11	\paragraph{Binary Synthetic Text Detection.}
12	Detection methods---DetectGPT~\cite{mitchell2023detectgpt}, Fast-DetectGPT~\citep{bao2024fast}, Binoculars~\cite{hans2024binoculars}, DivEye~\cite{basani2026diveye}, Min-K\%++~\cite{zhang2024minkpp}, CoDeC~\cite{zawalski2026codec}---achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate.
13	DivEye's surprisal diversity features overlap with our \texttt{surp\_*} subset, but optimize for binary detection rather than ordinal depth discrimination.
14	Sadasivan et al.~\cite{sadasivan2023detect} showed theoretical limits of binary detection under paraphrasing; Krishna et al.~\cite{krishna2023paraphrasing} demonstrated that paraphrasing evades detectors while retrieval provides a defense---relevant to our rewrite-mode analysis.
15	Hao et al.~\cite{hao2025rewrite} addressed rewrite-mode transfer for binary detection.
16	Recent work moves beyond binary labels along other axes: collaboration degree~\cite{cheng2025beyond} and contamination type hierarchies~\cite{mehta2025beyond}, but neither addresses recursive generation depth.
17	
18	\paragraph{Data Selection.}
19	DSIR~\cite{xie2023dsir}, D4~\cite{tirumala2023d4}, and DoReMi~\cite{xie2023doremi} improve corpus quality through distribution matching but ignore recursive depth.
20	Drayson et al.~\cite{drayson2025} combined binary detection with stratified importance resampling---a special case of our framework when $\Kstar = 1$.
21	Feng et al.~\cite{feng2025beyond} showed external verification can prevent collapse; our $\Kstar$ can inform such verifiers by delineating the depth frontier below which quality discrimination remains feasible.
22	
23	\paragraph{Ordinal Classification and Probing.}
24	Ordinal regression~\cite{frank2001ordinal, cao2020coral} exploits label ordering but has not been applied to synthetic text depth.
25	Our OBD head follows Frank \& Hall's binary decomposition principle.
26	Probing classifiers~\cite{alain2017understanding, conneau2018probing, belinkov2022probing} analyze pretrained representations; our neural probe follows this protocol with a controlled-capacity MLP on frozen embeddings.
27	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Extended Discussion}
2	\label{sec:extended_discussion}
3	
4	\subsection{Theoretical Implications of $\Kempirical = 2$}
5	
6	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees the existence of a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
7	Our empirical result---$\Kempirical = 2$ consistently across all 8 tested configurations---establishes that this boundary is remarkably shallow at the $\leq$1.5B scale.
8	
9	The shallowness is explained by the rate of information loss.
10	The 0v1 boundary is near-trivially separable ($\auc > 0.99$), reflecting the large distributional gap between human and first-generation text.
11	At 1v2, $\auc$ drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp (range: 0.6112--0.7039 across all conditions), indicating that the majority of discriminative signal is consumed in the first generation step.
12	By 2v3, $\auc$ falls to 0.5496--0.5917---below $\theta = 0.60$---and deeper pairs converge toward chance.
13	
14	This pattern aligns with model collapse findings.
15	Shumailov et al.~\cite{shumailov2024collapse} observed that tail-distribution degradation is most acute in the earliest generations.
16	Dohmatob et al.~\cite{dohmatob2024model} derived scaling laws demonstrating predictable tail probability mass loss.
17	Alemohammad et al.~\cite{alemohammad2024selfconsuming} proved convergence to a fixed point; $\Kempirical = 2$ indicates that operationally, this approach is rapid enough that adjacent-generation distinctions collapse within two steps.
18	
19	The consistency across decoding strategies merits emphasis.
20	Greedy, nucleus ($p{=}0.9$), $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$ all yield $\Kempirical = 2$ (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}), with 1v2 $\auc$ in $[0.6866, 0.7039]$.
21	Higher decoding entropy modestly increases signal strength but does not shift the boundary, suggesting that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the information bottleneck of the generation process rather than sampling algorithm properties.
22	
23	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer Implications}
24	
25	The cross-model transfer results (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) indicate that depth signals are largely encoded in text-level statistical properties rather than generator-specific artifacts.
26	Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retains 90--92\% accuracy, and broader continuation-mode transfer shows 80--92\% retention across all pairs.
27	At 0v1, cross-model $\auc$ exceeds 0.96 for all pairs, confirming fully transferable binary contamination detection.
28	
29	A single depth classifier trained on one model's outputs can be deployed for texts from other models without retraining---extending utility to open-web settings where the generating model is unknown.
30	
31	Two caveats: (1)~a nontrivial model-specific residual persists despite high transfer, and (2)~Qwen-continuation$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retention (80--85\%) is lower than Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo (90--92\%), indicating that model family similarity modulates transfer even among continuation-mode configurations.
32	
33	\subsection{Feature Importance Across Decoding Strategies}
34	
35	While $\Kempirical = 2$ is consistent across decoding strategies, feature importance ordering varies.
36	Greedy and $T{=}0.7$ share highly correlated rankings (Spearman $\rho = 0.914$), with vocabulary diversity measures dominating.
37	Greedy versus $T{=}1.2$ yields lower correlation ($\rho = 0.53$, $p = 0.041$; suggestive but nonsignificant after Bonferroni correction).
38	$T{=}1.2$ promotes \texttt{hapax\_ratio}, \texttt{ttr}, and \texttt{surp\_skew} to top ranks; \texttt{freq\_entropy} rises from rank~10 (greedy) to rank~5.
39	
40	The \emph{existence} of the depth boundary is robust, but the \emph{optimal feature subset} depends on sampling strategy.
41	A general-purpose classifier should incorporate features from multiple groups.
42	
43	\subsection{Scale Considerations}
44	
45	Our $\Kempirical = 2$ at $\leq$1.5B constitutes a methodological lower bound.
46	All 15 features are computed from surface-level token statistics without model internals, and cross-model transfer (80--92\%) demonstrates methodology generalization across architectures.
47	
48	Scaling to larger models presents two outcomes: if $\Kempirical > 2$ at 7B+ (as scaling laws suggest~\cite{dohmatob2024model}), depth contamination becomes more insidious and our framework more necessary; if $\Kempirical = 2$ persists, our conclusions generalize directly.
49	The high cross-model transfer across three architecturally distinct families provides indirect evidence that depth signals are not tied to a specific pretraining paradigm.
50	
51	\subsection{Cross-Model Neural Probe Analysis}
52	\label{sec:extended_neural_probe}
53	
54	The neural probe (frozen-encoder MLP on each model's own hidden states) yields model-dependent $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}$: Qwen-continuation achieves 2v3 $\auc = 0.6159$ $[0.610, 0.622]$, exceeding $\theta$ for $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$; Pythia-1.4B ($\auc_{2v3} = 0.5963$ $[0.589, 0.603]$) and OLMo-1B ($\auc_{2v3} = 0.5892$ $[0.582, 0.597]$) both fall below $\theta$, matching the 15D boundary at $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$.
55	
56	The Qwen-only elevation is consistent with a \emph{self-familiarity} effect: each probe uses the generator's own frozen embeddings to classify its own outputs.
57	Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, as an instruction-tuned model, encodes richer mode-specific artifacts in its hidden states that enable finer depth discrimination---but these artifacts do not transfer across model families.
58	By contrast, the 15D hand-crafted features yield $\Kempirical = 2$ universally across all three models, confirming that model-free statistical features provide a more robust and transferable depth signal.
59	
60	This result strengthens the practical case for model-free features: $\Ktheory \geq 3$ can only be claimed for Qwen representations specifically, while the model-free $\Kempirical = 2$ boundary holds as a feature-set-specific lower bound across all tested architectures.
61	
62	\subsection{LLM Zero-Shot Baseline}
63	\label{sec:extended_llm}
64	
65	On a 500-sample test set (5 depth classes), GPT-5.5 achieves 33.2\% accuracy (MAE = 1.10) and Claude Opus~4.6 achieves 33.1\% (MAE = 1.15)---both below OBD-LightGBM's 38.6\% (Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
66	Both achieve ${\sim}$88\% binary accuracy.
67	
68	Per-depth analysis: both models achieve high depth-0 recall (GPT-5.5: 0.77, Claude: 0.69) but recall for depths 1--4 collapses to 0.08--0.38, with predictions scattered nearly uniformly across non-human classes.
69	Spearman correlations are weak ($\rho = 0.38$, $0.42$), confirming that frontier LLMs function as binary detectors augmented by near-random depth guessing.
70	
71	Statistical detection methods (DetectGPT~\cite{mitchell2023detectgpt}, Binoculars~\cite{hans2024binoculars}) optimize scalar scores for the binary boundary---the same distinction where LLM baselines excel.
72	But the per-depth recall collapse demonstrates that binary detection confidence is orthogonal to generational depth.
73	
74	\subsection{Broader Impact}
75	
76	The practical consequence of $\Kempirical = 2$ is a three-tier taxonomy: human-authored (depth~0), first-generation synthetic (depth~1), and deeper synthetic (depth~$\geq 2$).
77	A single round of synthetic rewriting suffices to make text statistically indistinguishable from multiply recycled text---a one-wash laundering effect that limits filtering granularity.
78	
79	$\Kstar$ provides a corpus audit metric: what fraction of a training corpus consists of depth-$\geq 2$ text?
80	If prompt-chain depth signals serve as an observable proxy for early-stage model collapse, depth estimation could provide a lightweight diagnostic without expensive retraining experiments.
81	
82	\subsection{Degradation and Practical Considerations}
83	
84	In our continual training experiments (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}), all strategies degrade the base model's general capabilities (MMLU: 0.6018 $\to$ 0.5865--0.5941; HellaSwag: 0.6821 $\to$ 0.6638--0.6701).
85	This must be interpreted in context: our 100\% synthetic setting provides an upper-bound characterization.
86	In realistic mixed-corpus settings, degradation is proportional to the contamination fraction~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
87	Even under this extreme condition, $\Kstar$-aware binary filtering reduces MMLU degradation from $-$1.53\,pp to $-$0.77\,pp---an approximately 50\% reduction.
88	
89	Binary filtering at the $\Kstar = 2$ boundary requires no per-model calibration, is simple to implement, and is universally applicable across tested generator configurations.
90	Combining depth-aware filtering with distribution-matching methods (DSIR, DoReMi) is a promising direction.
91	
92	\subsection{Extended Limitations}
93	\label{sec:extended_limitations}
94	
95	\emph{Prompt-chain versus retrain-chain.}
96	Our prompt-chain protocol holds model quality constant; real-world retrain-chains introduce additional degradation through training dynamics.
97	Under the monotonic-degradation assumption~\cite{shumailov2024collapse}, $\Kstar^{\text{retrain}} \leq \Kstar^{\text{prompt}}$, making our $\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative estimate.
98	
99	\emph{Threshold sensitivity.}
100	At $\theta = 0.55$, $\Kempirical = 3$ uniformly.
101	At $\theta = 0.65$, continuation-mode configurations drop to $\Kempirical = 1$ while rewrite retains $\Kempirical = 2$.
102	At $\theta = 0.70$, $\Kempirical = 1$ universally.
103	The boundary is robust within a practical range but not invariant.
104	
105	\emph{Boundary marginality.}
106	Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound (0.6015) exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.15\,pp; Qwen-rewrite's 2v3 CI upper bound (0.5991) falls below $\theta$ by 0.09\,pp.
107	All eight boundary comparisons yield permutation $p < 0.01$.
108	
109	\emph{Graduated filtering.}
110	Graduated filtering at all tested $\alpha$ values (0.1--0.9) underperforms binary-ours on MMLU (best: $0.5892$ at $\alpha{=}0.9$ vs.\ $0.5941$), with a monotonic trend.
111	At $\alpha{=}0.9$, HellaSwag matches binary-ours exactly ($0.6701$), consistent with $\Kempirical = 2$ predicting no benefit from finer ordinal structure.
112	
113	\begin{table}[t]
114	\centering
115	\small
116	\caption{Graduated filtering $\alpha$ sweep (5 seeds, mean $\pm$ std). Higher $\alpha$ monotonically improves both benchmarks, converging toward binary-ours at $\alpha{=}0.9$.}
117	\label{tab:tab_alpha_sweep}
118	\input{tables/tab_alpha_sweep}
119	\end{table}
120	
```
